Let's look at the test: test_export_appends_totals_row expects export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]], totals=True) to return \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". So export_csv needs to support a totals keyword argument that appends a total row.\n\nCurrently export_csv just calls render_rows on the rows. We need to modify it to handle totals=True by computing column totals, appending a row marked as \"TOTAL\", and then rendering.\n\nLet's check the current export.py content again: it's just a simple function. We need to edit it.\n\nBut first, let's see if we can grep for export.py content or read it again. We already read it earlier. It shows:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\n\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nWe need to modify this function to accept totals=False parameter (or totals keyword) and logic.\n\nLet's edit export.py accordingly. We'll need to import column_totals from totals module.\n\nSo the new function might look like:\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    if totals and rows:\n        total_row = column_totals(rows)\n        rows.append(total_row)\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nBut wait, the test expects the output to be \"1,2\\n3,4\\nTOTAL,4,6\". Currently render_rows just joins values with commas. For the total row, we might need to prefix with \"TOTAL\"? Actually the test expects the last row to be \"TOTAL,4,6\". But render_rows as modified earlier (with mark_last) will produce \"TOTAL,\" + last_row_str when mark_last=True. However, in export_csv when totals=True, we want to append a total row that is rendered with \"TOTAL,\" prefix? Let's see test_render_marks_last_row_as_total: it tests render_rows with mark_last=True returns [\"1,2\", \"TOTAL,3,4\"] for input [[1, 2], [3, 4]]. So render_rows when mark_last=True will prefix the last row with \"TOTAL,\".\n\nBut in export_csv when totals=True, we want the appended total row to be like \"TOTAL,4,6\". However, note that the total row computed by column_totals for [[1,2],[3,4]] is [4,6]. If we then call render_rows on that row (which would produce \"4,6\"), and then we need to prefix with \"TOTAL,\"? Or maybe export_csv should call render_rows with mark_last=True on the combined rows? Actually the test test_export_appends_totals_row expects the result to have three rows: \"1,2\", \"3,4\", \"TOTAL,4,6\". So the last row is \"TOTAL,4,6\". This suggests that when totals=True, export_csv should:\n\n- Compute total row using column_totals(rows)\n- Append this total row to the rows list\n- Then render all rows, but for the last row (which is the total row), we want it to be prefixed with \"TOTAL,\"? Or maybe render_rows is called with mark_last=True on the entire set including the appended total row? But mark_last=True in render_rows marks the last row of the given rows as total by prefixing \"TOTAL,\". If we append the total row and then call render_rows(rows, mark_last=True), it would mark the last row (which is the newly appended total row) as total, resulting in \"TOTAL,4,6\" which is correct. But then the previous rows would not be marked. That seems right.\n\nAlternatively, we could manually construct the last row string as \"TOTAL,\" + comma-joined totals. But using render_rows with mark_last=True is cleaner given we already have that functionality.\n\nSo export_csv should:\n\n- If totals is True and rows is not empty:\n    - Compute totals_row = column_totals(rows)\n    - Append totals_row to rows\n    - Call render_rows(rows, mark_last=True)\n- Else:\n    - Call render_rows(rows)\n\nBut wait, the test test_export_without_totals expects export_csv([[1, 2], [3, 4]]) == \"1,2\\n3,4\". That's the default behavior without totals parameter. Currently export_csv doesn't take totals parameter. We need to add it with default False.\n\nAlso test_export_appends_totals_row expects call with totals=True.\n\nSo let's modify export_py accordingly.\n\nWe need to import column_totals from totals.\n\nLet's edit export.py using the edit tool. First, let's see the current content again via read, but we already have it. We'll do edit.\n\nWe need to replace the old function with new one.\n\nOld content:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\n\n\ndef export_csv(rows):\n    return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nNew content should be:\n\nfrom render import render_rows\nfrom totals import column_totals\n\n\ndef export_csv(rows, totals=False):\n    if totals and rows:\n        total_row = column_totals(rows)\n        rows.append(total_row)\n        return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows, mark_last=True))\n    else:\n        return \"\\n\".join(render_rows(rows))\n\nBut wait, render_rows expects mark_last parameter only when totals=True case. In the else case, we call render_rows(rows) which uses default mark_last=False.\n\nThis should satisfy the tests.\n\nLet's perform the edit.\n"}}
